## Your fixes
1. **Fakes can't drift from the real API.** CLI tests use hand-written payloads (`tests/cli/cli_fakes.py` and the test files). Add a check that every fake response body for a known endpoint validates against the real API response model (`mission_control.api.schemas.*`: `MissionSummaryOut` — expect it to include `owner_name: str`, so add that to the model in your check expectations only if it's already there; otherwise validate with the field allowed — `MissionOut`, `InboxOut`, `MatchPreviewOut`, `AssignmentOut`, `AssignmentSummaryOut`, crew/admin/auth models, etc.) with unknown fields forbidden, e.g. a helper in `cli_fakes.ok()`/`FakeApi.on()` keyed by method+path, or a parametrised test over the fixture payloads. Fix any fake that doesn't match the real models (e.g. inbox fakes, `owner_name`, skill/user fields). If a mismatch reveals a real CLI bug, fix the CLI.
2. **`mc crew set-skill` / `unset-skill` by Crew shows the wrong refusal.** `cli/commands/crew.py` (~88, ~105) resolves the skill through `GET /skills`, which Crew can't call, so Ben sees "Crew can't view the skill catalogue." Show the real reason instead (e.g. resolve from the target's `/crew/{id}` skills where possible, or catch the `role_forbidden` on the lookup and say "Only Mission Leads and Directors can set skill levels.").
3. **Piped password repeat.** `cli/prompts.py` (~49–66): without a terminal, `mc users add` / `mc password` expect the new password twice on stdin, so `echo pw | mc users add …` fails with "No password given…". Read the repeat only when interactive (as `mc-admin` does): non-interactive `password` reads current then new (2 lines), `users add` reads 1 line. Update tests and any help text.
4. **Sessions:** on `mc login`, if credentials already exist, best-effort `POST /auth/logout` with the old token before saving the new one (ignore failures). On `mc logout` when the server is unreachable, clear the local credentials anyway and warn ("Couldn't reach the server, so the session may stay valid until it expires. Removed it from this computer."), exit 0 or 4 — choose per cli-ux conventions and justify in the hand-off.
5. **Confirmation for `mc users deactivate`** (user approved): `! Deactivating Chen Wu can't be undone: they won't be able to log in. Deactivate Chen Wu? [y/N]`, `--yes`/`-y` to skip, same no-terminal behaviour as other confirmations (exit 2 with "Re-run with --yes").
6. **Don't confirm what the server will refuse:** `cli/commands/missions.py` (~107–117, ~749): skip the confirmation (and just send the request, letting the server refuse) when the caller isn't the mission's owner and isn't a Director — use `whoami`/the mission view's `owner_id` already fetched.
7. **Wording:** the `unauthenticated` hint duplicates "log in again" (`✗ Your session has expired or isn't valid; log in again.` + `Log in again: mc login`) → hint `Log in: mc login`. `admin.py:92` `Next: They log in with: mc login --email …` → `Next: mc login --email zed@aurora.example    (for Zed)`. Assignment statuses render lowercase (`proposed`) while mission statuses are title-case → map them the same way (`views.py`, `missions.py`). Make the unreachable-server hint use the same server command as the README (`uv run mc-server`) — check it already does.

---
name: cli-ux
description: Design or review the user experience of a command-line tool (CLI) or terminal UI (TUI). Use when planning commands, flags, help text, output, prompts or errors, when writing a CLI UX spec or mock terminal sessions, when reviewing CLI code or a CLI diff for usability, or when building interactive or full-screen terminal features (spinners, progress bars, prompts, raw mode, alternate screen, colors, resizing). Triggers on "CLI", "command line", "terminal UI", "TUI", "help text", "error message", "flags", "--json", "progress bar", "spinner", "interactive prompt", "Ctrl+C", "colors in terminal".
---

# CLI and TUI UX

Make terminal tools that are easy to learn, safe to script, and that leave the terminal as they found it.

## Two modes

**Designing.** Start from the user's workflows, not the command list:
1. Write the main workflows as mock terminal sessions: the exact commands typed and the exact output.
2. Apply the core rules below to every command and message.
3. Check the terminal mechanics in [references/terminal-mechanics.md](references/terminal-mechanics.md) for anything interactive, coloured or full-screen.
4. Run the checklist in [references/checklist.md](references/checklist.md) before calling the design done.

**Reviewing.** Read the code or diff and, where possible, run the tool for real, in a terminal and piped (`| cat`), with bad input and with Ctrl+C.
- Walk [references/checklist.md](references/checklist.md).
- Report findings using the output format below.

## Core rules

1. **Follow conventions.** Behave like the tools users already know. Use standard flags: `--help`, `--version`, `-q`, `-v`, `--json`, `--yes`. Use standard exit codes.
2. **Help everywhere.** `tool --help` lists every command with a one-line description, and `tool <cmd> --help` shows usage, arguments, options and an example.
3. **React to every action.** Success says what happened ("Logged out."). Silence is reserved for scriptable and quiet modes.
4. **Errors say what went wrong and how to fix it.** Never show a raw stack trace or back-end error. Name the thing involved and give the next command to run.
5. **Suggest the next step** at the end of common sequences (after `login`, suggest `create`).
6. **Flags over positional arguments** once there is more than one value. Give short forms to frequent flags.
7. **Prompt for missing required input** only when attached to a terminal, and offer sensible defaults. When not interactive, fail with a clear error instead of hanging.
8. **Show progress** for anything over about a second: a spinner for unknown length, a bar or named steps for known length. Progress goes to stderr.
9. **Always an easy way out.** Ctrl+C stops cleanly, restores the terminal, and exits 130. Interactive screens say how to quit.
10. **Write for skimmers.** Paragraphs of at most 3 short sentences, lists over prose, and a few consistent icons or colours used only as markers.
11. **Pipe-safe output.** Data goes to stdout and messages to stderr. Colour, spinners and prompts only when stdout or stdin is a TTY. Honour `NO_COLOR`, and offer `--json` for machines.
12. **Never depend on colour alone** for meaning. Pair it with a word or symbol.

Details, rationale and source for each rule: [references/principles.md](references/principles.md). Before/after examples: [references/examples.md](references/examples.md).

## Review output format

Group findings by severity: **Blocker** (unsafe, or breaks scripts), **Major** (users get stuck), **Minor** (friction). Each finding gives:
- the command or file:line;
- what the user sees today (exact text);
- the rule it breaks;
- a concrete rewrite (exact text, or the code change).

End with what works well, so it isn't changed by accident.
# Review checklist

Run each item for real where you can: in a terminal, piped (`| cat`), with no TTY on stdin (`< /dev/null`), with `NO_COLOR=1`, with bad input, and with Ctrl+C. Mark each item ✓, ✗ (with a finding) or n/a.

## Discoverability
- [ ] `tool`, `tool --help` and `tool <cmd> --help` all work; each command has a one-line description and at least one example.
- [ ] `--version` works.
- [ ] Command and flag names are consistent: same verbs, same `noun verb` order, no near-synonyms.
- [ ] Unknown commands and flags give a suggestion, not just "invalid".

## Input
- [ ] More than one value means named flags, not positional order. Frequent flags have short forms.
- [ ] A missing required input prompts when interactive, and gives a clear error when not. It never hangs.
- [ ] Sensible defaults cover the common case.
- [ ] Secrets are never accepted as flags; they're prompted for with input hidden.
- [ ] Destructive actions ask for confirmation, and `--yes` skips it.
- [ ] Invalid input is rejected before any side effect, and the message says what's valid.

## Feedback and output
- [ ] Every command reports its result in words ("Mission 12 created"), and `--quiet` suppresses it.
- [ ] Anything over about 1 second shows a spinner, a bar or named steps, on stderr, and only on a TTY.
- [ ] The next likely command is suggested at the end of common flows.
- [ ] Text is skimmable: short paragraphs, aligned tables, values truncated by display width.
- [ ] Icons and colours are few, consistent, and never the only signal.

## Errors
- [ ] No raw stack traces or back-end errors without `--verbose` / `--debug`.
- [ ] Each error says what went wrong, names the objects involved, and says how to fix it (ideally the exact next command).
- [ ] Several problems are listed together, one per line.
- [ ] Errors go to stderr, with a documented non-zero exit code.

## Scripting
- [ ] stdout holds only data; messages and progress go to stderr.
- [ ] When piped, there are no colours, spinners or escape codes. `NO_COLOR` and `TERM=dumb` are honoured.
- [ ] `--json` output is stable, documented and complete.
- [ ] Exit codes are documented: 0 ok, 1 failed or refused, 2 usage, 130 interrupted.
- [ ] `tool list | head` exits quietly (no broken-pipe traceback).

## Exit and interruption
- [ ] Ctrl+C stops promptly, cleans up, restores the terminal and exits 130. A second Ctrl+C forces exit.
- [ ] Interactive and full-screen modes show how to quit.
- [ ] After any exit (normal, error, signal), the cursor is visible, echo works and the prompt isn't coloured.

## TUI-specific (if full-screen or raw mode)
- [ ] Uses the alternate screen and always leaves it. The final result is printed after leaving.
- [ ] Raw mode is restored on every exit path, including exceptions.
- [ ] Resizing (SIGWINCH) redraws correctly. Very small sizes degrade gracefully.
- [ ] Rendering doesn't flicker (only changed cells are drawn).
- [ ] Focus is visible and keyboard-movable. Mouse support, if any, is opt-in and copy/paste is still possible.
- [ ] Works with 16 colours. Truecolor only when detected.
# CLI UX: `mc missions match`

Mock sessions for the matcher preview, written with the `cli-ux` skill (design mode). The service
(`services/matching.preview`, M14) returns the data. The CLI (M16) renders it as shown here.
Seed data is PRD §9. Names are the seeded display names.

**Conventions used below**
- `stdout` gets the data: the heading, the table and the summary line. `stderr` gets refusals and the `Next:` hints.
- On a TTY, `✓` is green, `!` is yellow and `✗` is red. The symbol always carries the meaning, so colour is never the only signal. Piped output or `NO_COLOR` gives the same text with no escape codes.
- **Plain-text markers.** The CLI uses plain words instead of symbols when stdout's encoding isn't UTF-8, or when `NO_COLOR` is set or `TERM=dumb`. Everything else stays the same. The mapping is:

  | Symbol | Plain text |
  |---|---|
  | `✓` | `OK:` |
  | `!` | `Note:` |
  | `✗` | `Error:` |
  | `·` (heading separator) | `-` |
  | `–` (date range) | `-` |

- Dates use the friendlier M16 format (`1–10 Mar 2027`). `--json` uses ISO dates.
- **The `--apply` hint** is printed only when the preview's `can_apply` is true, which means the caller owns the mission. A Director's preview never shows it. The hint is marked *available after M12*. Until then the CLI doesn't offer the flag, and the hint isn't printed.
- **Candidates.** Crew who hold a proposed or accepted assignment on the mission are never suggested for it, and neither is anyone who declined, withdrew or was removed from it.

## Help

```
$ mc missions match --help
Usage: mc missions match MISSION_ID [--apply] [--json]

  Suggest crew for a draft mission's open places. Nothing is changed.
  Owner or Director.

Options:
  --apply   Propose the suggested crew (owner only; available after M12)
  --json    Print the suggestion as JSON

Example:
  mc missions match 1
```

## S2: a full suggestion (Lee, on a TTY)

```
$ mc missions match 1
Lunar Gateway Resupply · 1–10 Mar 2027 · Draft
✓ Crew suggested for all 3 open places. Nothing has been proposed yet.

  Slot                 Suggested   Workload   Proficiency   Pick
  Flight Engineer      Chen        5 days     3             2
  Mission Specialist   Dana        5 days     4             3
  Commander            Ana         0 days     9             1

  Slots with the fewest eligible crew are filled first. Within a slot, lowest workload
  wins, then highest proficiency (sum of the required skill levels).

Next: mc missions match 1 --apply    proposes Ana, Chen and Dana (available after M12)
```
Exit `0`.

**Ruth (Director)** sees the same heading, summary line and table. There is no `Next:` line, because `can_apply` is false for her.

## S3: an unfilled place (Nora)

```
$ mc missions match 2
Asteroid Survey · 5–12 Mar 2027 · Draft
! Crew suggested for 1 of 2 open places. Geologist has no eligible crew left.

  Slot        Suggested   Workload   Proficiency   Pick
  Pilot       Ana         0 days     5             1
  Geologist   unfilled (1 place)

  The mission can't be submitted until every place is filled.

Next: mc missions match 2 --apply    proposes Ana for Pilot (available after M12)
      mc missions propose 2 --slot Geologist --crew EMAIL    fill Geologist by hand
```
Exit `0`: the preview worked. The unfilled place is reported, but it isn't an error.

## Refused: the mission isn't a draft (Lee)

The message is the lifecycle's own refusal (`state_problem(status, PREVIEW_MATCH)`). The CLI adds the mission ID, which it already knows, and a hint for each code.

```
$ mc missions match 5
✗ Mission 5: Can't suggest crew for an active mission.
  Only draft missions can be matched. See its crew: mc missions show 5
```
Exit `1`, on stderr. The other states:

| Code | Message | Hint |
|---|---|---|
| `invalid_transition` | `Can't suggest crew for a mission pending approval` | `Only draft missions can be matched. Its places are already filled: mc missions show 6` |
| `mission_final` | `Can't suggest crew for a mission that's completed` (or `cancelled`) | `Only draft missions can be matched. See it: mc missions show 7` |

## Refused: not allowed

```
$ mc missions match 1          # Ben (Crew), who isn't on Gateway
✗ Mission 1 not found.
  See your missions: mc missions list

$ mc missions match 1          # Nora (Lead), who doesn't own Gateway
✗ Only the owner or a Director can see suggested crew for this mission.
  See who owns it: mc missions show 1
```
Exit `1`, on stderr. Ben's refusal looks exactly like an unknown ID (TR-10).

## Piped

```
$ mc missions match 1 | cat
Lunar Gateway Resupply · 1–10 Mar 2027 · Draft
✓ Crew suggested for all 3 open places. Nothing has been proposed yet.

  Slot                 Suggested   Workload   Proficiency   Pick
  Flight Engineer      Chen        5 days     3             2
  Mission Specialist   Dana        5 days     4             3
  Commander            Ana         0 days     9             1

  Slots with the fewest eligible crew are filled first. Within a slot, lowest workload
  wins, then highest proficiency (sum of the required skill levels).
```
There are no escape codes. The `Next:` hint went to stderr, so it shows on the terminal but not in `> file`.

With `NO_COLOR`, `TERM=dumb` or a non-UTF-8 terminal, the markers become plain words:

```
$ NO_COLOR=1 mc missions match 2
Asteroid Survey - 5-12 Mar 2027 - Draft
Note: Crew suggested for 1 of 2 open places. Geologist has no eligible crew left.
...
$ NO_COLOR=1 mc missions match 5
Error: Mission 5: Can't suggest crew for an active mission.
```

## `--json`

```
$ mc missions match 2 --json
{
  "mission": {"id": 2, "name": "Asteroid Survey", "start_date": "2027-03-05", "end_date": "2027-03-12"},
  "can_apply": true,
  "open_places": 2,
  "unfilled_places": 1,
  "slots": [
    {"slot_id": 4, "name": "Pilot", "open_places": 1, "unfilled": 0,
     "suggested": [{"crew_profile_id": 6, "name": "Ana", "workload": 0, "proficiency": 5, "pick": 1}]},
    {"slot_id": 5, "name": "Geologist", "open_places": 1, "unfilled": 1, "suggested": []}
  ]
}
```
The JSON mirrors `MatchPreview`:
- `slots` covers every slot, in listed order. A slot that's already full has `open_places: 0`.
- `suggested` is in pick order.
- `workload` is in mission-days.
- `can_apply` is true only for the mission's owner.

A refusal prints the design §7 error body instead, with the same exit code.

## Exit codes

| Code | When |
|---|---|
| `0` | Preview shown, including when some places are unfilled |
| `1` | Refused: not found, not allowed, or the mission isn't a draft |
| `2` | Usage, e.g. `mc missions match abc` |
| `3` | Not logged in, or the session expired |
| `4` | Server unreachable or busy |
| `130` | Interrupted |

## Checklist (design review)

✓ means the design covers it, ✗ means there's a gap for M16, and n/a means it doesn't apply.

| Item | | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Help with an example | ✓ | Shown above |
| Flags over positionals | ✓ | Only one positional, the mission ID |
| A reaction for every action | ✓ | The summary line, including "Nothing has been proposed yet" |
| Next step suggested | ✓ | The `--apply` hint follows `can_apply`, so it's shown to the owner only. ✗ `--apply` itself waits for M12. |
| Skimmable, aligned table | ✓ | One row per place. A slot with several places gets one row per person. |
| Colour never the only signal | ✓ | `✓ ! ✗` plus words, with plain-text markers (`OK:` / `Note:` / `Error:`) when the output isn't UTF-8 or `NO_COLOR` / `TERM=dumb` is set |
| Errors name the object and the fix | ✓ | The CLI prefixes the mission ID, and each code has a hint. ✗ The domain wording doesn't name the mission. ✗ The 403 message doesn't name the owner. |
| Several problems listed together | n/a | Preview refusals carry one problem. For a single-problem refusal, the CLI must print the message once, not repeat it as a bullet. |
| stdout data, stderr messages | ✓ | |
| No escape codes when piped; `NO_COLOR` | ✓ | |
| Stable `--json` | ✓ | ✗ Still to decide for all commands in M16: does a refusal's JSON body go to stdout or stderr? |
| Documented exit codes | ✓ | |
| `\| head` without a broken-pipe traceback | ✗ | Applies to every command. Handle it in M16. |
| Progress | n/a | A single fast request |
| Prompts and confirmations | n/a | A preview writes nothing. `--apply` doesn't need a prompt, since crew still have to accept. |
| Ctrl+C exits 130 | ✓ | Listed in the exit codes |

"""Test doubles for the CLI: a scripted API behind `httpx2.MockTransport`, and a runner that
invokes `mc` in-process with its config directory under `tmp_path`.

Responses are shaped like the design's HTTP contract: JSON bodies, and every error as
`{"error": {"code", "message", "problems": [...]}}`.
"""

import json
from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping, Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import httpx2
from typer.testing import CliRunner

from mission_control.cli.config import Credentials, CredentialStore
from mission_control.cli.env import Env
from mission_control.cli.main import app

BASE_URL = "http://mc.test"
PREFIX = "/api/v1"

Handler = Callable[[httpx2.Request], httpx2.Response]


def ok(body: Any = None, status: int = 200) -> httpx2.Response:
    if body is None and status == 204:
        return httpx2.Response(204)
    return httpx2.Response(status, json=body)


def problem(code: str, message: str, **refs: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return {"code": code, "message": message, "refs": refs}


def error(
    status: int, code: str, message: str, problems: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]] | None = None
) -> httpx2.Response:
    listed = list(problems) if problems else [problem(code, message)]
    return httpx2.Response(
        status, json={"error": {"code": code, "message": message, "problems": listed}}
    )


@dataclass
class Call:
    method: str
    path: str
    params: dict[str, str]
    body: Any
    token: str | None


@dataclass
class FakeApi:
    """Routes `(method, path)` to a fixed response or a handler. Unrouted calls fail the test
    loudly with a 599, so a missing route can't pass by accident."""

    routes: dict[tuple[str, str], Handler | httpx2.Response] = field(default_factory=dict)
    calls: list[Call] = field(default_factory=list)

    def on(self, method: str, path: str, response: Handler | httpx2.Response) -> None:
        self.routes[(method.upper(), path)] = response

    def handle(self, request: httpx2.Request) -> httpx2.Response:
        path = request.url.path.removeprefix(PREFIX)
        body = json.loads(request.content) if request.content else None
        auth = request.headers.get("authorization", "")
        token = auth.removeprefix("Bearer ") if auth.startswith("Bearer ") else None
        self.calls.append(Call(request.method, path, dict(request.url.params), body, token))
        route = self.routes.get((request.method, path))
        if route is None:
            return httpx2.Response(599, json={"unrouted": f"{request.method} {path}"})
        return route(request) if callable(route) else route

    def client(self) -> httpx2.Client:
        return httpx2.Client(transport=httpx2.MockTransport(self.handle), base_url=BASE_URL)

    def sent(self, method: str, path: str) -> list[Call]:
        return [c for c in self.calls if c.method == method and c.path == path]

    def writes(self) -> list[Call]:
        return [c for c in self.calls if c.method != "GET"]


@dataclass
class ScriptedPrompter:
    """Answers prompts from a list and records each one as (label, hidden). Running out of
    answers behaves like Ctrl+C."""

    answers: list[str]
    asked: list[tuple[str, bool]] = field(default_factory=list)

    def ask(self, label: str) -> str:
        self.asked.append((label, False))
        return self._next()

    def ask_hidden(self, label: str) -> str:
        self.asked.append((label, True))
        return self._next()

    def _next(self) -> str:
        if not self.answers:
            raise KeyboardInterrupt
        return self.answers.pop(0)


@dataclass
class Result:
    exit_code: int
    stdout: str
    stderr: str
    exception: BaseException | None
    asked: list[tuple[str, bool]] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class Mc:
    """Runs `mc` in-process against a FakeApi, with credentials kept under a temp directory."""

    api: FakeApi
    config_dir: Path

    @property
    def store(self) -> CredentialStore:
        return CredentialStore(self.config_dir)

    def login_as(self, token: str = "tok-lee", email: str = "lee@aurora.example") -> None:
        self.store.save(Credentials(token=token, email=email, expires_at="2027-01-05T21:00:00Z"))

    def __call__(
        self,
        *args: str,
        input: str | None = None,
        answers: Sequence[str] | None = None,
        color: bool | None = False,
        http: httpx2.Client | None = None,
    ) -> Result:
        """`answers` makes the run interactive and answers its prompts in order. Without it
        there's no terminal, and `input` is what's piped to stdin."""
        prompter = ScriptedPrompter(list(answers or ()))
        env = Env(
            http=http or self.api.client(),
            config_dir=self.config_dir,
            interactive=answers is not None,
            color=color,
            prompter=prompter,
        )
        result = CliRunner().invoke(app, list(args), obj=env, input=input, prog_name="mc")
        return Result(
            result.exit_code, result.stdout, result.stderr, result.exception, prompter.asked
        )

    record.update(extra)

## 7. API

**Conventions**
- REST and JSON, under `/api/v1`.
- Authentication with the header `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
- Lifecycle changes are explicit `POST` commands, for example `/submit`. They are never `PATCH status`, because each one has its own guards and audit event ([TR-8](#tr-8)).
- List endpoints return only what the caller is allowed to see.

#### Auth and me
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/auth/login` | Exchange email and password for a token |
| POST | `/auth/logout` | Revoke the current token |
| GET | `/me` | Current user, role and crew profile |
| POST | `/me/password` | Change password (revokes all sessions) |
| GET | `/me/inbox` | Things waiting on me: a to-do list computed from current data (see below) |

#### Organisation, users and skills (Directors)
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET · PATCH | `/org` | View settings, or set the required approvals |
| GET · POST | `/users` | List users, or create one with an initial password |
| PATCH | `/users/{id}` | Change role or deactivate |
| POST | `/users/{id}/crew-profile` | Give a user a crew profile |
| GET · POST | `/skills` | List skills (Leads too), or add one |
| PATCH · DELETE | `/skills/{id}` | Rename, or delete if unused |

#### Crew
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/crew` · `/crew/{id}` | List or view profiles |
| PATCH | `/crew/{id}` | Edit own display name and bio |
| PUT · DELETE | `/crew/{id}/skills/{skill_id}` | Set or remove a skill level |
| GET · POST | `/crew/{id}/unavailability` | List or add own unavailability |
| DELETE | `/crew/{id}/unavailability/{uid}` | Remove own unavailability |
| GET | `/crew/{id}/assignments` | Assignment history (summaries) |

#### Missions
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET · POST | `/missions` | List visible missions, or create one with slots |
| GET · PATCH | `/missions/{id}` | View, or edit name and dates |
| POST | `/missions/{id}/slots` | Add a slot |
| PATCH · DELETE | `/missions/{id}/slots/{sid}` | Edit or remove a slot |
| GET | `/missions/{id}/history` | Audit trail |
| POST | `/missions/{id}/submit` · `approve` · `reject` · `complete` · `cancel` | Lifecycle commands |
| POST | `/missions/{id}/owner` | Transfer ownership |
| GET | `/missions/{id}/match` | Preview suggested roster |
| POST | `/missions/{id}/match/apply` | Create proposals from the suggestion |

#### Assignments
| Method | Path | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/missions/{id}/assignments` | Propose a crew member for a slot |
| GET | `/assignments` | My assignments |
| POST | `/assignments/{id}/accept` · `decline` · `withdraw` · `remove` | Respond or remove |

#### Inbox

`GET /me/inbox` (`mc inbox`) lists only what is waiting on the caller to act. It's computed from current data on each request, so nothing is stored, nothing is marked as read, and an item disappears once it's dealt with. Every role has one inbox, showing whichever sections apply:

| Section | Shown to | Item | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| Proposals | Anyone with a crew profile | Their own Proposed assignments on Draft missions | accept or decline |
| Approvals | Directors | Pending missions they're an eligible approver for and haven't approved this round | approve or reject |
| Missions | The owning Lead | Their Draft missions, each with what's waiting: places to fill (open places, including after a decline, withdrawal or rejection), proposals awaiting replies, or "ready to submit" | staff or submit |

Not shown: pending missions waiting on others, Active missions, and anything informational. Each section is a few indexed lookups of the caller's own rows (`assignments_profile`, `missions_org_status`, `missions_owner`); the missions section shows counts only and leaves the full eligibility check to submit.

#### Errors

Every error has the same shape, for every status code (401, 403, 404, 409, 422, 503). `problems` is always present and always has at least one entry, so clients only ever handle one format. A rule or state conflict (409) lists **every** problem at once. A rule refusal's code is always `rule_violation`, with a summary message ("Ben can't be proposed as Flight Engineer on Lunar Gateway Resupply"); each reason is a problem with its own code. Only a state conflict has a code of its own (one of the **State** codes below), repeated as its single problem. A permission refusal (403) lists its single reason, because only one applies and the actor can't fix it themselves. Each problem names the record involved:

```json
{
  "error": {
    "code": "rule_violation",
    "message": "Mission can't be submitted",
    "problems": [
      { "code": "slot_unfilled", "message": "Commander: 1 of 1 place unfilled", "refs": { "slot_id": 12 } }
    ]
  }
}
```

| HTTP | Meaning | Codes |
|---|---|---|
| 401 | Not authenticated | `invalid_credentials` `unauthenticated` |
| 403 | Not allowed | `role_forbidden` `not_owner` `approver_is_creator` `approver_is_rostered` `self_skill_edit` `wrong_current_password` |
| 404 | Not found or not visible | `not_found` |
| 409 | Conflicts with state or a rule | **State:** `mission_frozen` `mission_final` `invalid_transition` `already_decided`<br>**Rules:** `rule_violation`, with the reasons as problem codes (below) |
| 422 | Invalid input | `validation_error` `password_too_short` |
| 503 | Database busy, retry | `busy` |

Malformed mission and slot values are 422 `validation_error`, and every mistake is listed at once: a blank mission or slot name, a headcount below 1, a slot with no required skill, a minimum level outside 1–5, an end date before the start date, or a Director creating a mission without naming its owner.

**Problem codes.** These appear inside `problems` and explain each refusal. Adding one means updating this table in the same change.

| Code | Raised when | Typical `refs` |
|---|---|---|
| `inactive` | The person is deactivated | `crew_profile_id`, `slot_id` |
| `skill_below_minimum` | A required skill is missing or below the minimum | `crew_profile_id`, `slot_id`, `skill_id` |
| `unavailable` | Unavailability overlaps the mission's dates | `crew_profile_id`, `slot_id` |
| `overlap` | An accepted assignment on another mission overlaps (the message names that mission) | `crew_profile_id`, `mission_id`, `mission_name` |
| `slot_has_open_assignments` | Removing a slot that still has crew | `assignment_id`, `crew_profile_id`, `slot_id` |
| `headcount_below_open_assignments` | Lowering headcount below the assigned crew (the message names them) | `slot_id`, `crew_profile_ids` |
| `invalidates_active_assignment` | A skill change would break an assignment on an Active mission | `mission_id`, `skill_id` |
| `slot_unfilled` | Submitting with empty places | `slot_id` |
| `proposal_unanswered` | Submitting while a proposal is still waiting for a reply (the message names the person and slot) | `assignment_id`, `slot_id`, `crew_profile_id` |
| `already_exists` | Inside a 409 `rule_violation`: the email address is already in use (anywhere on the platform, since login is by email alone; see [TR-13](#tr-13)), a skill name is already in the catalogue (ignoring case), the user already has a crew profile, or a mission already has a live slot with that name (ignoring case; a new mission's slots are also checked against each other, and have no `slot_id` yet) | `email`; `skill_id`; `user_id`, `crew_profile_id`; `slot_id` |
| `owner_not_active_lead` | The owner named for a new mission, or for a transfer, isn't a Mission Lead or is deactivated (inside a 409 `rule_violation`) | `user_id` |
| `insufficient_approvers` | Submitting when fewer eligible approvers exist than the approvals required | — |
| `has_open_assignments` | Deactivating someone who still holds proposed or accepted assignments | `user_id`, `mission_ids` |
| `owns_open_missions` | Deactivating, or changing the role of, a Lead who owns unfinished missions | `user_id`, `mission_ids` |
| `last_director` | Deactivating or demoting the organisation's only active Director | `user_id` |
| `skill_in_use` | Deleting a skill that crew hold or slots require (the message names them) | `skill_id`, `crew_profile_ids`, `mission_ids` |
| `mission_needs_a_slot` | Removing a mission's only slot | `slot_id` |
| `already_owner` | Transferring a mission to the Lead who already owns it | `user_id` |
| `slot_full` | Proposing someone for a slot with no open places (the message names the slot and mission) | `slot_id` |
| `already_assigned` | Proposing someone who already holds an open assignment on this mission (the message names their slot) | `assignment_id`, `crew_profile_id`, `mission_id` |

---

## 8. CLI

| Concern | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Client | An `httpx2` wrapper passed into commands, so tests use `TestClient` and need no server |
| Auth | Token stored in `~/.config/mission-control/credentials.json` (mode 0600). Cleared on logout, password change or 401. |
| References | Missions and assignments by ID. People by email. Skills by name. Slots by name or position. |
| Passwords | Hidden prompt only, never a flag. Asked twice when setting one. |
| Output | Plain tables. No meaning carried by colour alone. `--json` for scripts. |
| Errors | The message, then one bullet per problem, then a hint for known codes |
| Exit codes | `0` OK · `1` refused · `2` usage · `3` authentication · `4` unreachable or busy |
| Confirmations | Changing a Pending mission, or cancelling, asks first. `--yes` skips the prompt. |

```bash
# Everyone
mc login | logout | whoami | password | inbox
mc me show | update | unavailable add|list|remove
mc assignments list [--pending] | accept ID | decline ID | withdraw ID --reason R

# Leads
mc missions create --name N --start D --end D --slot "Commander:1:Piloting>=4,Leadership>=3"
mc missions edit | slot add|edit|remove | match ID [--apply]
mc missions propose ID --slot S --crew EMAIL | remove-crew ID ASSIGNMENT | submit ID
mc crew list | show | history | set-skill EMAIL SKILL LEVEL | unset-skill EMAIL SKILL

# Directors
mc missions approve ID | reject ID --reason R | transfer ID --owner EMAIL
mc missions complete ID | cancel ID --reason R | list | show | history
mc users list | add | set-role | deactivate | enable-crew
mc skills list | add | rename | remove          mc org show | set-approvals N
```

"""Request and response bodies. Requests check shape and type only; every business rule, and
every check with a domain message (blank names, levels out of range), stays in the services.
Responses are built explicitly from what the services return, computed values included."""
from pydantic import BaseModel, field_validator, model_validator

from mission_control.api.schemas.common import Int, RequestBody
from mission_control.domain.enums import Role
from mission_control.infra.repositories.models import Organisation, Skill, User

# Organisation


class OrgOut(BaseModel):
    id: int
    name: str
    slug: str
    required_approvals: int

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, org: Organisation) -> "OrgOut":
        return cls(
            id=org.id, name=org.name, slug=org.slug, required_approvals=org.required_approvals
        )


class OrgUpdate(RequestBody):
    required_approvals: Int


# Users


class UserOut(BaseModel):
    id: int
    email: str
    name: str
    role: Role
    active: bool

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, user: User) -> "UserOut":
        return cls(
            id=user.id, email=user.email, name=user.name, role=user.role, active=user.is_active
        )


class UserCreate(RequestBody):
    email: str
    name: str
    role: Role
    password: str


class UserUpdate(RequestBody):
    """A new role, or `active: false` to deactivate. Both are separate use cases, each in its
    own transaction, so one request makes only one of them."""

    role: Role | None = None
    active: bool | None = None

    @field_validator("active")
    @classmethod
    def _only_deactivate(cls, active: bool | None) -> bool | None:
        if active is True:
            raise ValueError("can only be false: deactivated users can't be reactivated")
        return active

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _one_change(self) -> "UserUpdate":
        if self.role is not None and self.active is not None:
            raise ValueError("Change a user's role and deactivate them in separate requests")
        return self


class CrewProfileCreate(RequestBody):
    display_name: str | None = None


# Skills


class SkillOut(BaseModel):
    id: int
    name: str
    category: str | None

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, skill: Skill) -> "SkillOut":
        return cls(id=skill.id, name=skill.name, category=skill.category)


class SkillCreate(RequestBody):
    name: str
    category: str | None = None


class SkillUpdate(RequestBody):
    """A field left out stays as it is; `category: null` clears the category."""

    name: str | None = None
    category: str | None = None

from mission_control.api.schemas.common import Int, RequestBody

class Proposal(RequestBody):

from datetime import datetime

from pydantic import BaseModel

from mission_control.api.schemas.admin import UserOut
from mission_control.api.schemas.common import RequestBody
from mission_control.services.auth import Me, Session


class Login(RequestBody):
    email: str
    password: str


class SessionOut(BaseModel):
    token: str  # shown once; send it as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`
    expires_at: datetime
    user: UserOut

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, session: Session) -> "SessionOut":
        return cls(
            token=session.token, expires_at=session.expires_at, user=UserOut.of(session.user)
        )


class OrganisationRef(BaseModel):
    id: int
    name: str
    slug: str


class MeOut(UserOut):
    """The caller's own account, organisation and crew profile."""

    crew_profile_id: int | None
    organisation: OrganisationRef

    @classmethod
    def of_me(cls, me: Me) -> "MeOut":
        org = me.organisation
        return cls(
            **UserOut.of(me.user).model_dump(),
            crew_profile_id=me.crew_profile_id,
            organisation=OrganisationRef(id=org.id, name=org.name, slug=org.slug),
        )


class PasswordChange(RequestBody):
    current_password: str
    new_password: str
from typing import Annotated

from fastapi import Path
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field

# SQLite stores 64-bit integers. Anything larger is malformed input, refused as 422 rather than
# failing in the database.
_MIN_INT, _MAX_INT = -(2**63), 2**63 - 1

Int = Annotated[int, Field(ge=_MIN_INT, le=_MAX_INT)]
PathId = Annotated[int, Path(ge=_MIN_INT, le=_MAX_INT)]


class RequestBody(BaseModel):
    """A request body. Unknown fields are refused, so a misspelt field isn't silently ignored."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")
from datetime import date

from pydantic import BaseModel

from mission_control.api.schemas.common import Int, RequestBody
from mission_control.domain.enums import AssignmentStatus, MissionStatus
from mission_control.infra.repositories.models import AssignmentSummary, CrewProfile, Unavailability
from mission_control.services.crew import CrewView, SkillLevel, UnavailabilityView


class SkillLevelOut(BaseModel):
    skill_id: int
    skill_name: str
    level: int

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, s: SkillLevel) -> "SkillLevelOut":
        return cls(skill_id=s.skill_id, skill_name=s.skill_name, level=s.level)


class UnavailabilityOut(BaseModel):
    id: int
    start_date: date
    end_date: date
    reason: str | None  # null when the caller may not see it

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, u: UnavailabilityView | Unavailability) -> "UnavailabilityOut":
        return cls(id=u.id, start_date=u.window.start, end_date=u.window.end, reason=u.reason)


class CrewOut(BaseModel):
    crew_profile_id: int
    user_id: int
    name: str
    email: str
    active: bool
    bio: str | None
    skills: list[SkillLevelOut]
    unavailability: list[UnavailabilityOut]

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, v: CrewView) -> "CrewOut":
        return cls(
            crew_profile_id=v.crew_profile_id,
            user_id=v.user_id,
            name=v.name,
            email=v.email,
            active=v.active,
            bio=v.bio,
            skills=[SkillLevelOut.of(s) for s in v.skills],
            unavailability=[UnavailabilityOut.of(u) for u in v.unavailability],
        )


class CrewProfileOut(BaseModel):
    crew_profile_id: int
    user_id: int
    name: str
    bio: str | None

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, p: CrewProfile) -> "CrewProfileOut":
        return cls(crew_profile_id=p.id, user_id=p.user_id, name=p.display_name, bio=p.bio)


class CrewUpdate(RequestBody):
    """A field left out (or null) stays as it is; a blank bio clears it."""

    display_name: str | None = None
    bio: str | None = None


class SkillLevelSet(RequestBody):
    level: Int


class UnavailabilityAdd(RequestBody):
    start_date: date
    end_date: date
    reason: str


class AssignmentSummaryOut(BaseModel):
    """One line of someone's assignment history. It doesn't give access to the mission."""

    assignment_id: int
    mission_id: int
    mission_name: str
    start_date: date
    end_date: date
    mission_status: MissionStatus
    slot_name: str
    status: AssignmentStatus
    reason: str | None

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, a: AssignmentSummary) -> "AssignmentSummaryOut":
        return cls(
            assignment_id=a.assignment_id,
            mission_id=a.mission_id,
            mission_name=a.mission_name,
            start_date=a.window.start,
            end_date=a.window.end,
            mission_status=a.mission_status,
            slot_name=a.slot_name,
            status=a.status,
            reason=a.reason,
        )

from pydantic import BaseModel

from mission_control.api.schemas.crew import AssignmentSummaryOut
from mission_control.services.inbox import ApprovalItem, Inbox, MissionItem, UnfilledSlot


class ApprovalItemOut(BaseModel):
    mission_id: int
    mission_name: str
    start_date: date
    end_date: date
    approvals: int
    required: int

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, a: ApprovalItem) -> "ApprovalItemOut":
        return cls(
            mission_id=a.mission_id,
            mission_name=a.mission_name,
            start_date=a.window.start,
            end_date=a.window.end,
            approvals=a.approvals,
            required=a.required,
        )


class UnfilledSlotOut(BaseModel):
    slot_id: int
    slot_name: str
    open_places: int

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, u: UnfilledSlot) -> "UnfilledSlotOut":
        return cls(slot_id=u.slot_id, slot_name=u.slot_name, open_places=u.open_places)


class MissionItemOut(BaseModel):
    mission_id: int
    mission_name: str
    start_date: date
    end_date: date
    unfilled: list[UnfilledSlotOut]
    unanswered: int
    ready: bool  # every place filled and every proposal accepted

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, m: MissionItem) -> "MissionItemOut":
        return cls(
            mission_id=m.mission_id,
            mission_name=m.mission_name,
            start_date=m.window.start,
            end_date=m.window.end,
            unfilled=[UnfilledSlotOut.of(u) for u in m.unfilled],
            unanswered=m.unanswered,
            ready=m.ready,
        )


class InboxOut(BaseModel):
    """What is waiting on the caller: proposals to answer, missions to approve, and their own
    draft missions to staff or submit."""

    proposals: list[AssignmentSummaryOut]
    approvals: list[ApprovalItemOut]
    missions: list[MissionItemOut]

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, box: Inbox) -> "InboxOut":
        return cls(
            proposals=[AssignmentSummaryOut.of(p) for p in box.proposals],
            approvals=[ApprovalItemOut.of(a) for a in box.approvals],
            missions=[MissionItemOut.of(m) for m in box.missions],
        )

from pydantic import BaseModel

from mission_control.services.matching import MatchPreview, SlotSuggestion, SuggestedCrew


class MatchMissionOut(BaseModel):
    id: int
    name: str
    start_date: date
    end_date: date


class SuggestedCrewOut(BaseModel):
    crew_profile_id: int
    name: str
    workload: int  # mission-days
    proficiency: int
    pick: int

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, s: SuggestedCrew) -> "SuggestedCrewOut":
        return cls(
            crew_profile_id=s.crew_profile_id,
            name=s.name,
            workload=s.workload,
            proficiency=s.proficiency,
            pick=s.pick,
        )


class SlotSuggestionOut(BaseModel):
    slot_id: int
    name: str
    open_places: int
    unfilled: int
    suggested: list[SuggestedCrewOut]  # in pick order

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, s: SlotSuggestion) -> "SlotSuggestionOut":
        return cls(
            slot_id=s.slot_id,
            name=s.name,
            open_places=s.open_places,
            unfilled=s.unfilled,
            suggested=[SuggestedCrewOut.of(p) for p in s.suggested],
        )


class MatchPreviewOut(BaseModel):
    mission: MatchMissionOut
    can_apply: bool  # only the mission's owner may turn the suggestion into proposals
    open_places: int
    unfilled_places: int
    slots: list[SlotSuggestionOut]  # every slot, in listed order

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, p: MatchPreview) -> "MatchPreviewOut":
        return cls(
            mission=MatchMissionOut(
                id=p.mission_id,
                name=p.mission_name,
                start_date=p.window.start,
                end_date=p.window.end,
            ),
            can_apply=p.can_apply,
            open_places=p.open_places,
            unfilled_places=p.unfilled_places,
            slots=[SlotSuggestionOut.of(s) for s in p.slots],
        )
from collections.abc import Mapping
from datetime import date, datetime

from pydantic import BaseModel, field_validator, model_validator

from mission_control.api.schemas.common import Int, RequestBody
from mission_control.domain.enums import AssignmentStatus, MissionStatus
from mission_control.domain.records import Requirement
from mission_control.infra.repositories.models import AuditEntry, Mission
from mission_control.services.missions import (
    ApprovalView,
    AssignmentView,
    MissionView,
    SlotInput,
    SlotView,
)

# Requests


class RequirementIn(RequestBody):
    skill_id: Int
    min_level: Int


def _levels(requirements: list[RequirementIn]) -> dict[int, int]:
    return {r.skill_id: r.min_level for r in requirements}


def _no_repeats(requirements: list[RequirementIn] | None) -> list[RequirementIn] | None:
    seen: set[int] = set()
    for r in requirements or ():
        if r.skill_id in seen:
            raise ValueError(f"lists skill {r.skill_id} more than once")
        seen.add(r.skill_id)
    return requirements


class SlotIn(RequestBody):
    name: str
    headcount: Int
    requirements: list[RequirementIn]

    _unique = field_validator("requirements")(_no_repeats)

    def to_input(self) -> SlotInput:
        return SlotInput(self.name, self.headcount, _levels(self.requirements))


class MissionCreate(RequestBody):
    name: str
    start_date: date
    end_date: date
    slots: list[SlotIn]
    owner_id: Int | None = None  # Directors name the Lead; Leads own what they create


class MissionUpdate(RequestBody):
    name: str | None = None
    start_date: date | None = None
    end_date: date | None = None


class SlotUpdate(RequestBody):
    """Rename or change headcount, or replace the required skills. Each is its own use case and
    transaction, so one request makes only one kind of change."""

    name: str | None = None
    headcount: Int | None = None
    requirements: list[RequirementIn] | None = None

    _unique = field_validator("requirements")(_no_repeats)

    @model_validator(mode="after")
    def _one_kind(self) -> "SlotUpdate":
        if self.requirements is not None and (self.name is not None or self.headcount is not None):
            raise ValueError(
                "Change a slot's required skills separately from its name and headcount"
            )
        return self

    def levels(self) -> dict[int, int] | None:
        return None if self.requirements is None else _levels(self.requirements)


class OwnerChange(RequestBody):
    owner_id: Int


class ReasonIn(RequestBody):
    reason: str | None = None  # required to reject or cancel; the service says so if it's missing


# Responses


class RequirementOut(BaseModel):
    skill_id: int
    skill_name: str
    min_level: int

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, r: Requirement) -> "RequirementOut":
        return cls(skill_id=r.skill_id, skill_name=r.skill_name, min_level=r.min_level)


class SlotOut(BaseModel):
    id: int
    position: int
    name: str
    headcount: int
    open_places: int
    requirements: list[RequirementOut]

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, s: SlotView) -> "SlotOut":
        return cls(
            id=s.id,
            position=s.position,
            name=s.name,
            headcount=s.headcount,
            open_places=s.open_places,
            requirements=[RequirementOut.of(r) for r in s.requirements],
        )


class RosterAssignmentOut(BaseModel):
    id: int
    slot_id: int
    slot_name: str
    crew_profile_id: int
    crew_name: str
    status: AssignmentStatus
    reason: str | None

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, a: AssignmentView) -> "RosterAssignmentOut":
        return cls(
            id=a.id,
            slot_id=a.slot_id,
            slot_name=a.slot_name,
            crew_profile_id=a.crew_profile_id,
            crew_name=a.crew_name,
            status=a.status,
            reason=a.reason,
        )


class ApprovalOut(BaseModel):
    director_id: int
    director_name: str
    decision: str
    reason: str | None

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, a: ApprovalView) -> "ApprovalOut":
        return cls(
            director_id=a.director_id,
            director_name=a.director_name,
            decision=a.decision,
            reason=a.reason,
        )


class MissionSummaryOut(BaseModel):
    id: int
    name: str
    start_date: date
    end_date: date
    status: MissionStatus
    owner_id: int
    created_by_id: int
    approval_round: int
    approvals_required: int | None  # fixed when the mission is submitted

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, m: Mission) -> "MissionSummaryOut":
        return cls(**_summary(m))


class MissionOut(MissionSummaryOut):
    """A mission with its live slots in listed order, every assignment it has had, and the
    approvals of its current round."""

    owner_name: str
    created_by_name: str
    slots: list[SlotOut]
    assignments: list[RosterAssignmentOut]
    approvals: list[ApprovalOut]

    @classmethod
    def of_view(cls, v: MissionView) -> "MissionOut":
        return cls(
            **_summary(v.mission),
            owner_name=v.owner_name,
            created_by_name=v.created_by_name,
            slots=[SlotOut.of(s) for s in v.slots],
            assignments=[RosterAssignmentOut.of(a) for a in v.assignments],
            approvals=[ApprovalOut.of(a) for a in v.approvals],
        )


def _summary(m: Mission) -> dict[str, object]:
    return {
        "id": m.id,
        "name": m.name,
        "start_date": m.window.start,
        "end_date": m.window.end,
        "status": m.status,
        "owner_id": m.owner_id,
        "created_by_id": m.created_by_id,
        "approval_round": m.approval_round,
        "approvals_required": m.approvals_required,
    }


class HistoryEntryOut(BaseModel):
    """One event in a mission's history: who did what, when, and the names at the time."""

    id: int
    created_at: datetime
    actor_id: int
    actor_name: str
    event: str
    assignment_id: int | None
    crew_profile_id: int | None
    from_status: str | None
    to_status: str | None
    reason: str | None
    data: Mapping[str, object]

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, e: AuditEntry) -> "HistoryEntryOut":
        return cls(
            id=e.id,
            created_at=e.at,
            actor_id=e.actor_id,
            actor_name=e.actor_name,
            event=e.event,
            assignment_id=e.assignment_id,
            crew_profile_id=e.crew_profile_id,
            from_status=e.from_status,
            to_status=e.to_status,
            reason=e.reason,
            data=dict(e.data),
        )

"""Missions: create and edit them, crew them (by hand or with the matcher), and take them
through submission, approval, completion or cancellation."""

import shlex
from collections.abc import Sequence
from contextlib import AbstractContextManager
from typing import Annotated, Any

import typer

from mission_control.cli.client import Api
from mission_control.cli.commands.common import (
    Json,
    MissionId,
    Yes,
    command,
    group,
    iso_date,
    start,
)
from mission_control.cli.env import Env
from mission_control.cli.errors import Refused, UsageProblem
from mission_control.cli.prompts import confirm
from mission_control.cli.refusals import refusals
from mission_control.cli.render import Span, Terminal, format_timestamp, join_names, plural
from mission_control.cli.resolve import (
    SLOT_EXAMPLE,
    crew_by_email,
    parse_requirements,
    parse_slot_spec,
    requirement_body,
    skill_ids,
    slot_in,
    user_by_email,
)
from mission_control.cli.views import mission_heading, mission_row, requirements_text

app = group("Create, crew and run missions.")
slot_app = group("Add, change or remove a mission's slots.")
app.add_typer(slot_app, name="slot")

SlotRef = Annotated[
    str, typer.Argument(metavar="SLOT", help="The slot's name, or its position (1 for the first)")
]
Reason = Annotated[
    str, typer.Option("--reason", metavar="TEXT", help="Why (recorded in the history)")
]
StartDate = Annotated[
    str, typer.Option("--start", metavar="YYYY-MM-DD", callback=iso_date, help="First day")
]
EndDate = Annotated[
    str, typer.Option("--end", metavar="YYYY-MM-DD", callback=iso_date, help="Last day")
]
NewName = Annotated[str | None, typer.Option("--name", metavar="NAME", help="A new name")]
NewStart = Annotated[
    str | None,
    typer.Option("--start", metavar="YYYY-MM-DD", callback=iso_date, help="A new first day"),
]
NewEnd = Annotated[
    str | None,
    typer.Option("--end", metavar="YYYY-MM-DD", callback=iso_date, help="A new last day"),
]
Headcount = Annotated[
    int | None, typer.Option("--headcount", metavar="N", help="How many people it needs")
]
Requires = Annotated[
    str | None,
    typer.Option("--requires", metavar="SKILL>=LEVEL,...", help="Replace its required skills"),
]
SlotSpec = Annotated[
    str,
    typer.Option(
        "--slot", metavar="SPEC", help=f"NAME:HEADCOUNT:SKILL>=LEVEL,... e.g. {SLOT_EXAMPLE}"
    ),
]
SlotSpecs = Annotated[
    list[str],
    typer.Option(
        "--slot", metavar="SPEC", help=f"NAME:HEADCOUNT:SKILL>=LEVEL,... e.g. {SLOT_EXAMPLE}"
    ),
]


def _refusals(mission_id: int, **hints: str) -> AbstractContextManager[None]:
    return refusals(
        f"Mission {mission_id}",
        name_state=True,
        not_found="See your missions: mc missions list",
        not_owner=f"See who owns it: mc missions show {mission_id}",
        **hints,
    )


def _invalidated_hints(mission_id: int) -> dict[str, str]:
    """Hints for an edit refused because it would invalidate crew's assignments."""
    return {
        "slot_has_open_assignments": (
            f"Remove crew first: mc missions remove-crew {mission_id} {{assignment_id}}"
        ),
        "headcount_below_open_assignments": f"See who is assigned: mc missions show {mission_id}",
        "skill_below_minimum": f"See who is assigned: mc missions show {mission_id}",
        "unavailable": f"See who is assigned: mc missions show {mission_id}",
        "overlap": f"See who is assigned: mc missions show {mission_id}",
    }


def _confirm_if_pending(env: Env, mission: dict[str, Any], doing: str, yes: bool) -> bool:
    """Ask before a change that sends a pending mission back to Draft. True if it was pending."""
    if mission["status"] != "pending_approval":
        return False
    confirm(
        env,
        f"{mission['name']} is pending approval. {doing} sends it back to Draft and clears its "
        "approvals.",
        yes=yes,
    )
    return True


def _back(was_pending: bool) -> str:
    return " It's back in Draft." if was_pending else ""


def _done(env: Env, json_output: bool, body: Any, message: str) -> None:
    if json_output:
        env.term.json(body)
    else:
        env.term.ok(message)


def _slot_body(api: Api, specs: Sequence[str]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    parsed = [parse_slot_spec(s) for s in specs]
    ids = skill_ids(api, [name for spec in parsed for name, _ in spec.requirements])
    return [
        {
            "name": s.name,
            "headcount": s.headcount,
            "requirements": requirement_body(s.requirements, ids),
        }
        for s in parsed
    ]


# Create, list, show, history


@command(
    app,
    "create",
    example=(
        'mc missions create --name "Europa Flyby" --start 2027-09-01 --end 2027-09-20 '
        f'--slot "{SLOT_EXAMPLE}"'
    ),
)
def create(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    name: Annotated[str, typer.Option("--name", metavar="NAME", help="The mission's name")],
    start_date: StartDate,
    end_date: EndDate,
    slots: SlotSpecs,
    owner: Annotated[
        str | None,
        typer.Option(
            "--owner", metavar="EMAIL", help="The owning Lead (Directors create for a Lead)"
        ),
    ] = None,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Create a draft mission with one or more slots. Repeat --slot for each."""
    for spec in slots:
        parse_slot_spec(spec)  # report a malformed spec before anything is sent
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    body: dict[str, Any] = {
        "name": name,
        "start_date": start_date,
        "end_date": end_date,
        "slots": _slot_body(api, slots),
    }
    if owner is not None:
        body["owner_id"] = user_by_email(api, owner)["id"]
    with refusals():
        mission = api.post("/missions", body)
    if json_output:
        env.term.json(mission)
        return
    env.term.ok(f"Created mission {mission['id']}: {mission_heading(env.term, mission)}")
    env.term.next_steps([f"mc missions match {mission['id']}"])


@command(app, "list", example="mc missions list")
def list_missions(ctx: typer.Context, json_output: Json = False) -> None:
    """List the missions you can see."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    missions = env.api().get("/missions")
    if json_output:
        env.term.json(missions)
        return
    if not missions:
        env.term.line("No missions to show.")
        return
    with_owner = any(m.get("owner_name") for m in missions)
    headers = ["ID", "Mission", "Dates", "Status"] + (["Owner"] if with_owner else [])
    env.term.table(
        headers,
        [
            mission_row(env.term, m) + ([m.get("owner_name") or ""] if with_owner else [])
            for m in missions
        ],
    )


@command(app, "show", example="mc missions show 1")
def show(ctx: typer.Context, mission_id: MissionId, json_output: Json = False) -> None:
    """Show a mission: its slots, crew and approvals."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        mission = env.api().get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
    if json_output:
        env.term.json(mission)
        return
    _show_mission(env.term, mission)


def _show_mission(term: Terminal, mission: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    term.line(mission_heading(term, mission))
    owner = f"Owner {mission.get('owner_name') or mission['owner_id']}"
    if mission.get("created_by_name") and mission.get("created_by_id") != mission.get("owner_id"):
        owner = term.heading(owner, f"created by {mission['created_by_name']}")
    term.line(owner)
    term.line()
    term.table(
        ["#", "Slot", "Requires", "Places"],
        [
            [
                str(s["position"]),
                s["name"],
                requirements_text(s["requirements"]),
                "full" if s["open_places"] <= 0 else f"{s['open_places']} of {s['headcount']} open",
            ]
            for s in mission["slots"]
        ],
    )
    term.line()
    crew = mission.get("assignments") or []
    if crew:
        term.table(
            ["Assignment", "Crew", "Slot", "Status"],
            [[str(a["id"]), a["crew_name"], a["slot_name"], a["status"]] for a in crew],
        )
    else:
        term.line("No crew proposed yet.")
    required = mission.get("approvals_required")
    if mission["status"] in ("pending_approval", "active", "completed") and required:
        approvers = [
            a["director_name"] for a in mission.get("approvals") or [] if a["decision"] == "approve"
        ]
        names = f" ({join_names(approvers)})" if approvers else ""
        term.line()
        term.line(f"Approvals: {len(approvers)} of {required}{names}")


_EVENTS: dict[str, tuple[str, str]] = {
    # event: (with the names from its data, without them)
    "mission.created": ("Created the mission", "Created the mission"),
    "mission.updated": ("Edited the mission", "Edited the mission"),
    "mission.submitted": ("Submitted for approval", "Submitted for approval"),
    "mission.approved": ("Approved", "Approved"),
    "mission.rejected": ("Rejected", "Rejected"),
    "mission.activated": ("Mission became active", "Mission became active"),
    "mission.completed": ("Completed", "Completed"),
    "mission.cancelled": ("Cancelled", "Cancelled"),
    "mission.transferred": ("Handed the mission to {to_owner_name}", "Changed the owner"),
    "mission.returned_to_draft": ("Mission went back to Draft", "Mission went back to Draft"),
    "slot.added": ("Added slot {slot_name}", "Added a slot"),
    "slot.updated": ("Changed slot {slot_name}", "Changed a slot"),
    "slot.requirements_changed": ("Changed the skills for {slot_name}", "Changed a slot's skills"),
    "slot.removed": ("Removed slot {slot_name}", "Removed a slot"),
    "assignment.proposed": ("Proposed {crew_name} for {slot_name}", "Proposed crew"),
    "assignment.accepted": ("Accepted {slot_name}", "Accepted a proposal"),
    "assignment.declined": ("Declined {slot_name}", "Declined a proposal"),
    "assignment.withdrawn": ("Withdrew from {slot_name}", "Withdrew"),
    "assignment.removed": ("Removed {crew_name} from {slot_name}", "Removed crew"),
    "assignment.completed": ("{crew_name}'s assignment completed", "Assignment completed"),
    "assignment.cancelled": ("{crew_name}'s assignment cancelled", "Assignment cancelled"),
}


def _event_text(entry: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    named, plain = _EVENTS.get(entry["event"], (entry["event"], entry["event"]))
    try:
        return named.format_map(entry.get("data") or {})
    except (KeyError, IndexError, ValueError):
        return plain


@command(app, "history", example="mc missions history 1")
def history(ctx: typer.Context, mission_id: MissionId, json_output: Json = False) -> None:
    """Show who changed a mission, what they did and when."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        entries = env.api().get(f"/missions/{mission_id}/history")
    if json_output:
        env.term.json(entries)
        return
    if not entries:
        env.term.line("Nothing has happened to this mission yet.")
        return
    with_reasons = any(e.get("reason") for e in entries)
    env.term.table(
        ["When (UTC)", "Who", "What"] + (["Reason"] if with_reasons else []),
        [
            [
                format_timestamp(e["created_at"]),
                e.get("actor_name") or str(e.get("actor_id")),
                _event_text(e),
            ]
            + ([e.get("reason") or ""] if with_reasons else [])
            for e in entries
        ],
    )


# Edit and slots


@command(app, "edit", example="mc missions edit 1 --end 2027-03-12")
def edit(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    name: NewName = None,
    start_date: NewStart = None,
    end_date: NewEnd = None,
    yes: Yes = False,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Change a mission's name or dates. Owner only.

    A mission pending approval goes back to Draft, so you're asked first.
    """
    body = {"name": name, "start_date": start_date, "end_date": end_date}
    body = {k: v for k, v in body.items() if v is not None}
    if not body:
        raise UsageProblem("Nothing to change. Pass --name, --start or --end.")
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    with _refusals(mission_id, **_invalidated_hints(mission_id)):
        mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
        was_pending = _confirm_if_pending(env, mission, "Editing it", yes)
        updated = api.patch(f"/missions/{mission_id}", body)
    _done(env, json_output, updated, f"Updated {updated['name']}.{_back(was_pending)}")


@command(slot_app, "add", example='mc missions slot add 1 --slot "Geologist:1:Geology>=4"')
def add_slot(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    spec: SlotSpec,
    yes: Yes = False,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Add a slot to a mission. Owner only."""
    parse_slot_spec(spec)
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
        body = _slot_body(api, [spec])[0]
        was_pending = _confirm_if_pending(env, mission, "Adding a slot", yes)
        updated = api.post(f"/missions/{mission_id}/slots", body)
    _done(
        env, json_output, updated, f"Added {body['name']} to {mission['name']}.{_back(was_pending)}"
    )


@command(
    slot_app,
    "edit",
    example='mc missions slot edit 1 Commander --headcount 2 --requires "Piloting>=5"',
)
def edit_slot(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    slot: SlotRef,
    name: NewName = None,
    headcount: Headcount = None,
    requires: Requires = None,
    yes: Yes = False,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Rename a slot, change its headcount or replace its required skills. Owner only."""
    requirements = parse_requirements(requires) if requires is not None else None
    if name is None and headcount is None and requirements is None:
        raise UsageProblem("Nothing to change. Pass --name, --headcount or --requires.")
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    with _refusals(mission_id, **_invalidated_hints(mission_id)):
        mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
        target = slot_in(mission, slot)
        body: dict[str, Any] = {}
        if name is not None:
            body["name"] = name
        if headcount is not None:
            body["headcount"] = headcount
        if requirements is not None:
            ids = skill_ids(api, [n for n, _ in requirements])
            body["requirements"] = requirement_body(requirements, ids)
        was_pending = _confirm_if_pending(env, mission, "Changing a slot", yes)
        updated = api.patch(f"/missions/{mission_id}/slots/{target['id']}", body)
    _done(
        env,
        json_output,
        updated,
        f"Updated {target['name']} on {mission['name']}.{_back(was_pending)}",
    )


@command(slot_app, "remove", example='mc missions slot remove 1 "Mission Specialist"')
def remove_slot(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    slot: SlotRef,
    yes: Yes = False,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Remove a slot. Owner only. Refused while anyone is assigned to it."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    with _refusals(mission_id, **_invalidated_hints(mission_id)):
        mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
        target = slot_in(mission, slot)
        was_pending = _confirm_if_pending(env, mission, "Removing a slot", yes)
        updated = api.delete(f"/missions/{mission_id}/slots/{target['id']}")
    _done(
        env,
        json_output,
        updated,
        f"Removed {target['name']} from {mission['name']}.{_back(was_pending)}",
    )


# Crew


@command(app, "match", example="mc missions match 1")
def match(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    apply: Annotated[
        bool, typer.Option("--apply", help="Propose the suggested crew (owner only)")
    ] = False,
    json_output: Annotated[
        bool, typer.Option("--json", help="Print the suggestion as JSON")
    ] = False,
) -> None:
    """Suggest crew for a draft mission's open places. Nothing is changed.

    Owner or Director.
    """
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    hints = {
        "mission_frozen": (
            f"Only draft missions can be matched. See its crew: mc missions show {mission_id}"
        ),
        "invalid_transition": (
            "Only draft missions can be matched. Its places are already filled: "
            f"mc missions show {mission_id}"
        ),
        "mission_final": (
            f"Only draft missions can be matched. See it: mc missions show {mission_id}"
        ),
    }
    with _refusals(mission_id, **hints):
        if apply:
            _apply(env, api, mission_id, json_output)
            return
        preview = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}/match")
    if json_output:
        env.term.json(preview)
        return
    _show_preview(env.term, mission_id, preview)


def _show_preview(term: Terminal, mission_id: int, preview: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    mission = preview["mission"]
    term.line(
        term.heading(
            mission["name"], term.dates(mission["start_date"], mission["end_date"]), "Draft"
        )
    )
    slots = [s for s in preview["slots"] if s["open_places"] > 0]
    open_places = preview["open_places"]
    unfilled = [s for s in slots if s["unfilled"] > 0]
    suggested = sorted(
        ((p, s) for s in slots for p in s["suggested"]), key=lambda pair: pair[0]["pick"]
    )
    if open_places == 0:
        term.ok("Every place already has someone proposed or accepted. Nothing to suggest.")
        return
    if not unfilled:
        places = "the 1 open place" if open_places == 1 else f"all {open_places} open places"
        term.ok(f"Crew suggested for {places}. Nothing has been proposed yet.")
    else:
        names = join_names([s["name"] for s in unfilled])
        verb = "has" if len(unfilled) == 1 else "have"
        filled = open_places - preview["unfilled_places"]
        term.note(
            f"Crew suggested for {filled} of {plural(open_places, 'open place')}. "
            f"{names} {verb} no eligible crew left."
        )
    term.line()
    rows: list[list[str] | Span] = []
    for slot in slots:
        for person in slot["suggested"]:
            rows.append(
                [
                    slot["name"],
                    person["name"],
                    plural(person["workload"], "day"),
                    str(person["proficiency"]),
                    str(person["pick"]),
                ]
            )
        if slot["unfilled"]:
            rows.append(Span(slot["name"], f"unfilled ({plural(slot['unfilled'], 'place')})"))
    term.table(["Slot", "Suggested", "Workload", "Proficiency", "Pick"], rows)
    term.line()
    if unfilled:
        term.line("  The mission can't be submitted until every place is filled.")
    else:
        term.line(
            "  Slots with the fewest eligible crew are filled first. Within a slot, lowest workload"
        )
        term.line("  wins, then highest proficiency (sum of the required skill levels).")

    if not preview.get("can_apply"):
        return
    steps = []
    if suggested:
        if len(suggested) == 1:
            person, slot = suggested[0]
            who = f"{person['name']} for {slot['name']}"
        else:
            who = join_names([p["name"] for p, _ in suggested])
        steps.append(f"mc missions match {mission_id} --apply    proposes {who}")
    for slot in unfilled:
        steps.append(
            f"mc missions propose {mission_id} --slot {shlex.quote(slot['name'])} --crew EMAIL    "
            f"fill {slot['name']} by hand"
        )
    term.next_steps(steps)


def _apply(env: Env, api: Api, mission_id: int, json_output: bool) -> None:
    proposed = api.post(f"/missions/{mission_id}/match/apply")
    if json_output:
        env.term.json(proposed)
        return
    if not proposed:
        env.term.note("No crew could be proposed: no eligible crew for the open places.")
        return
    mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
    by_id = {a["id"]: a for a in mission.get("assignments") or []}
    env.term.ok(
        f"Proposed {len(proposed)} crew for {mission['name']}. "
        f"{'They need' if len(proposed) == 1 else 'Each of them needs'} to accept."
    )
    env.term.line()
    rows = []
    for item in proposed:
        known = by_id.get(item["id"], {})
        rows.append([str(item["id"]), known.get("crew_name", ""), known.get("slot_name", "")])
    env.term.table(["Assignment", "Crew", "Slot"], rows)
    env.term.next_steps([f"mc missions show {mission_id}"])


@command(
    app,
    "propose",
    example='mc missions propose 1 --slot "Flight Engineer" --crew chen@aurora.example',
)
def propose(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    slot: Annotated[
        str, typer.Option("--slot", metavar="SLOT", help="The slot's name or position")
    ],
    crew: Annotated[str, typer.Option("--crew", metavar="EMAIL", help="The crew member's email")],
    yes: Yes = False,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Propose a crew member for a slot. Owner only. They still need to accept."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    free = f"See who is free: mc missions match {mission_id}"
    hints = {
        "overlap": free,
        "unavailable": free,
        "skill_below_minimum": free,
        "inactive": free,
        "slot_full": f"See the open places: mc missions show {mission_id}",
    }
    with _refusals(mission_id, **hints):
        mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
        target = slot_in(mission, slot)
        person = crew_by_email(api, crew)
        was_pending = _confirm_if_pending(env, mission, "Proposing crew", yes)
        assignment = api.post(
            f"/missions/{mission_id}/assignments",
            {"slot_id": target["id"], "crew_profile_id": person["crew_profile_id"]},
        )
    _done(
        env,
        json_output,
        assignment,
        f"Proposed {person['name']} for {target['name']} on {mission['name']} "
        f"(assignment {assignment['id']}). They need to accept.{_back(was_pending)}",
    )


@command(app, "remove-crew", example="mc missions remove-crew 1 21")
def remove_crew(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    assignment_id: Annotated[
        int, typer.Argument(metavar="ASSIGNMENT_ID", help="From mc missions show")
    ],
    yes: Yes = False,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Remove someone from a mission, or take back a proposal. Owner only."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
        held = next((a for a in mission.get("assignments") or [] if a["id"] == assignment_id), None)
        if held is None:
            raise Refused(
                f"Assignment {assignment_id} isn't on {mission['name']}",
                hint=f"See its crew: mc missions show {mission_id}",
            )
        was_pending = _confirm_if_pending(env, mission, "Removing crew", yes)
        result = api.post(f"/assignments/{assignment_id}/remove")
    _done(
        env,
        json_output,
        result,
        f"Removed {held['crew_name']} from {held['slot_name']} on {mission['name']}."
        f"{_back(was_pending)}",
    )


# Lifecycle


@command(app, "submit", example="mc missions submit 1")
def submit(ctx: typer.Context, mission_id: MissionId, json_output: Json = False) -> None:
    """Submit a fully crewed draft for approval. Owner only."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    hints = {
        "slot_unfilled": f"Fill the open places: mc missions match {mission_id}",
        "proposal_unanswered": f"See who hasn't answered: mc missions show {mission_id}",
    }
    with _refusals(mission_id, **hints):
        mission = env.api().post(f"/missions/{mission_id}/submit")
    needs = mission.get("approvals_required")
    after = f" It needs {plural(needs, 'approval')}." if needs else ""
    _done(env, json_output, mission, f"Submitted {mission['name']} for approval.{after}")


@command(app, "approve", example="mc missions approve 1")
def approve(ctx: typer.Context, mission_id: MissionId, json_output: Json = False) -> None:
    """Approve a mission pending approval. Directors who didn't create it and aren't on it."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        mission = env.api().post(f"/missions/{mission_id}/approve")
    if mission["status"] == "active":
        state = "It's now active."
    else:
        given = sum(1 for a in mission.get("approvals") or [] if a["decision"] == "approve")
        needed = plural(mission["approvals_required"], "approval")
        state = f"It's pending approval ({given} of {needed})."
    _done(env, json_output, mission, f"Approved {mission['name']}. {state}")


@command(app, "reject", example='mc missions reject 6 --reason "Roster too thin"')
def reject(
    ctx: typer.Context, mission_id: MissionId, reason: Reason, json_output: Json = False
) -> None:
    """Reject a mission pending approval. It goes back to Draft. Directors only."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        mission = env.api().post(f"/missions/{mission_id}/reject", {"reason": reason})
    _done(env, json_output, mission, f"Rejected {mission['name']}. It's back in Draft.")


@command(app, "transfer", example="mc missions transfer 1 --owner nora@aurora.example")
def transfer(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    owner: Annotated[str, typer.Option("--owner", metavar="EMAIL", help="The new owning Lead")],
    yes: Yes = False,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Hand a mission to another Lead. Directors only."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        new_owner = user_by_email(api, owner)
        mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
        was_pending = _confirm_if_pending(env, mission, "Changing the owner", yes)
        updated = api.post(f"/missions/{mission_id}/owner", {"owner_id": new_owner["id"]})
    _done(
        env,
        json_output,
        updated,
        f"{updated['name']} now belongs to {new_owner['name']}.{_back(was_pending)}",
    )


@command(app, "complete", example="mc missions complete 5")
def complete(ctx: typer.Context, mission_id: MissionId, json_output: Json = False) -> None:
    """Mark an active mission completed. Owner or Director."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        mission = env.api().post(f"/missions/{mission_id}/complete")
    _done(
        env,
        json_output,
        mission,
        f"Completed {mission['name']}. Its accepted assignments are completed too.",
    )


@command(app, "cancel", example='mc missions cancel 1 --reason "Budget cut"')
def cancel(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    mission_id: MissionId,
    reason: Reason,
    yes: Yes = False,
    json_output: Json = False,
) -> None:
    """Cancel a mission that isn't finished. Owner or Director. Asks first."""
    env = start(ctx, json_output)
    api = env.api()
    with _refusals(mission_id):
        mission = api.get(f"/missions/{mission_id}")
        confirm(
            env,
            f"Cancelling {mission['name']} can't be undone. Its open assignments will be "
            "cancelled too.",
            yes=yes,
            question=f"Cancel {mission['name']}?",
        )
        cancelled = api.post(f"/missions/{mission_id}/cancel", {"reason": reason})
    _done(env, json_output, cancelled, f"Cancelled {cancelled['name']}.")
